# Preface.

When a man, without any formal education or upbringing, while also living in poverty and in a lowly station, driven solely by an inner urge for the knowledge of truth and by an earnest concern for the salvation of his soul, succeeds so far—that through constant reflection, prayer, and struggle, he attains a deep insight into religion, rises to original views, and presents them in writing in a naive style <note>In this context, "naive style" refers to a natural, unpretentious, and straightforward way of writing, typical of those without formal academic training.</note>, then he certainly deserves all respect because of it, and the unuse-

<summary>This preface introduces a self-taught man from a lower social class who, despite lack of education, achieves profound religious insight and original thought through spiritual struggle.</summary>
<keywords>education, religious insight, social class, spiritual struggle, salvation, self-taught, preface</keywords>

Such a man, however, is the misunderstood and despised **Jacob Böhme** <note>Jacob Böhme (1575–1624) was a German shoemaker and Christian mystic. Despite his humble origins, his complex visions regarding the nature of God and the origin of evil had a profound influence on later philosophy and Romanticism.</note>. It is true that his writings are full of mysticism, enthusiasm <note>original: "Schwärmerei" — in this period, this term specifically referred to a kind of fanatical or excessive religious zeal, often viewed with suspicion by rationalist thinkers.</note>, and nonsense, but only in the explanation of objective objects. In the **subjective, practical, religious, and moral** realms, he always speaks as a man of reason, character, and prudence. For the fact that he earnestly insists upon a life in God, and does not weaken it through any modifications or toning down of the divine in favor of [human] inclinations, truly reasonable men will not charge him with mysticism for that. Only in his theory of the **eternal Being and Becoming of God**, of the **creation of the world**, of the **Trinity**, of the **fall of the angels and of man**, and of the **origin of evil** is he mystical, but always in such a way that no practical errors can be derived from it. And if a conception occurs that could lead to practical errors, he seeks to—

He argued that evil is a result of the creature turning away from the light of God's love into the "dark fire" of their own self-will.</note>, and by deriving all evil solely from the **free will** of these creatures. Obscurities and contradictions certainly remain in his explanations of metaphysical <term>supersensible objects</term> <note>original: "übersinnlichen Objecte" — things that exist beyond the reach of the physical senses, such as the divine essence or the human soul.</note>, the reason for which lies partly in the nature of the subjects themselves, but partly and primarily in **Böhme’s** diverse and complex presentations of them. ...who grasped the idea of the **supersensible** <term>supersensible</term> <note>original: "Uebersinnlichen" — referring to things that exist beyond the reach of the physical senses, such as the divine essence or the human soul.</note> so deeply, and has presented it so powerfully. The rational and sober elements contained in his writings alongside the mystical revelations clearly show that no ordinary spirit dwelt within him. Furthermore, his persistent will—striving for the knowledge of truth and overcoming all obstacles—bears glorious witness to his spirit. Nothing was more interesting to him than the exploration of the Divine, and it is touching how often and lamentably he describes the painful state of mind in which he found himself when writing—and thus the most effective means of recording and continuing his revelations—was forbidden to him, and he felt bound to remain obedient to the official decree even in this matter. <note>In 1613, after the publication of his first work 'Aurora', Böhme was arrested and forbidden from writing further by the city council of Görlitz, under pressure from the local Lutheran pastor. He remained silent for several years before feeling a divine command to begin writing again.</note>

### 1. The Tower of Babel.

The Tower near the great city of Babel is an image of the fallen earthly man, who has entered into **selfhood** <term>selfhood</term> <note>original: "Selbstheit" — in Böhme’s mysticism, this refers to the egoistic state where the human will turns away from God and centers on itself.</note>, and has turned the **Word of God** within him into an idol. For the tower, which men built in their own conceit, was intended to stand as a great wonder, upon which they could climb to God; and it indicates how they had lost their understanding and their **knowledge** <term>knowledge</term> <note>original: "Erkenntniß" — implies spiritual insight or gnosis rather than mere intellectual learning.</note> of God and of His dwelling and essence. They had departed from the Spirit of God and sought to take the **Kingdom of God** for themselves through their own power in their selfhood; for the **confused languages** <note>original: "verwirrte Sprachen" — referring to the Confusion of Tongues in Genesis 11, interpreted here as the fragmentation of a single divine truth into many conflicting human perspectives.</note> indicate that every property or quality introduced itself into a selfhood and its own understanding, and for that reason they no longer understood one another. The Tower is the

<vocab>Tower of Babel, Selfhood, Word of God, Idol, Knowledge, Kingdom of God, Confused languages</vocab>

<summary>This page presents an allegorical interpretation of the Tower of Babel, viewing it not just as a historical building but as a symbol of humanity's fall into egoism and spiritual confusion. It suggests that the "confusion of tongues" represents the moment human reason became fragmented and separated from the unified Spirit of God.</summary>

<keywords>Tower of Babel, Babel, selfhood, Word of God, spiritual knowledge, Kingdom of God, confusion of tongues, Jakob Böhme, mysticism, ego</keywords>

self-chosen God and divine service. All the scholars of reason from the school of this world are the master builders of this tower. All those who set themselves up as teachers, and are called to it by men without God's Spirit, they are all foremen <note>original: "Werkmeister" — master craftsmen or supervisors; here referring to those who manage the construction of human-made religious systems.</note> on this tower and idols of the world, none other; they all together only carve stone and wood for this tower.

All people from Adam onwards, who have ever taught about God without being driven by the Spirit of God within them, they have all spoken and taught from this tower of confused tongues. And from this the dispute about God and His will and essence has arisen, that men have quarreled about God in their **selfhood** <term>selfhood</term> <note>original: "Selbstheit" — in Böhme’s mysticism, this refers to the egoistic state where the human will turns away from God and centers on itself.</note>; one said they should bring bricks for the building of the tower, another stones, the third lime, and so on, each something different; each wanted to build the tower according to the property <note>original: "Eigenschaft" — referring to the specific character or quality of one's nature.</note> of his own **selfhood**, so that all the world should see what he had built. And when the peoples of other lands saw what these had built, they despised it and said: their materials were better for the tower, and they built another for themselves, and praised it. But this one was again despised by others, and theirs held to be better, until they finally became so divided in **pride** <note>original: "Hoffart" — an archaic term for vanity or spiritual arrogance.</note> and in quarreling that they murdered and persecuted one another over the **knowledge** <term>knowledge</term> <note>original: "Erkenntniß" — implies spiritual insight or gnosis rather than mere intellectual learning.</note> of the Tower of Babel, and whichever party then conquered,

But within the immortal human being is Christ, who has broken through the **death of the letter** <note>original: "Tod der Buchstaben" — an allusion to 2 Corinthians 3:6, "the letter kills, but the Spirit gives life." The author argues that literalist, intellectual dogma is a form of spiritual death.</note> and has turned death into life. Now it is up to the individual to also die to the images of the letters within them, and to depart from all the scholarship of the **Babylonian builders**, by whatever name they may be called, and enter into the one life of Jesus. They should not dispute about that path—where it might be found—but only consider that it is within them, and that they must leave everything behind, whether it be **art** or **wit** <note>original: "Kunst oder Witz" — in this context, "art" refers to human skill or artifice, and "wit" refers to intellectual cleverness and worldly reason.</note>, and only become at one within themselves. They should give glory to God in all things and recognize that neither understanding, wisdom, nor wit belongs to us as our own, but that the **divine sun** shines out of and through us, and works within us as it wills.

By this, one should now recognize whether a person is a teacher sent from God, or whether they have come from the spirit of Babylonian images, letters, and opinions. If he is born of love, then he possesses the light of divine **knowledge** <term>knowledge</term> <note>original: "Erkenntniß" — in mystical tradition, this refers to a direct, experiential "gnosis" or spiritual insight, rather than mere book-learning.</note>, and speaks from the Spirit of God; he rebukes and teaches with power, without regard for any person's status, for he teaches from God’s Spirit and only that which the Spirit teaches within him.

But if he is a builder of the Tower of Babel, and born from the spirit of the letter—

[teaches], then he is a hypocrite and a flatterer of those who adorn him and help him honor his **Letter-God** <term>Letter-God: original "Buchstabengott," referring to a deity constructed from literalist dogma and intellectual study rather than living spiritual experience</term> in divided tongues; he is a mocker of those who do not honor him in his own form; one who is glory-seeking and proud under a glistening appearance, greedy and envious. He boasts of wisdom and understanding, yet is only a builder on the **Tower of Babel** and a **painted Christian** <note>original: "gemahlter Christ" — a common 17th-century term for a superficial or hypocritical believer who has only the appearance of piety</note>, and by his own power he forces words together into a single **opinion** <note>original: "Meinung" — in this context, it refers to a subjective, human-made sectarian belief or dogma as opposed to divine Truth</note>.

And those who flock to him and join themselves with him in this opinion are the city of **Babel**, like the children of **Nimrod** <note>The biblical figure Nimrod was traditionally associated with the construction of the Tower of Babel</note>, who wish to climb to heaven upon this tower; they climb forever, throughout their whole lives, yet they never reach heaven through their opinion. Instead, when the time of the literal **constellation** <note>referring to the astrological or providential timeframe governing the physical body and worldly systems</note> is over, the outer man falls, together with the built tower of opinion, and everything breaks apart together, except for the single soul, which then stands naked and bare before God.

Here there is now no counsel, unless the soul possesses within itself the **One Spirit** of the sounding letters of the **Living Word**, with which it may clothe itself; a Spirit that can shatter all images of the letters and lead them into a single tongue and a single will, which is God, All in All. For everything must return into the One, as into the Whole; in multiplicity there is only strife and unrest, but in the One there is eternal rest.

[God] and no resentment. The **human tree** <term>human tree: original "menschliche Baum," a metaphor suggesting that all of humanity is a single living organism rooted in the divine, despite outward differences</term> is but a single tree, and if they remained in the one God who created them, and did not make for themselves **images and opinions** <note>original: "Bilder und Meinungen" — refers to mental constructs, sectarian dogmas, and rigid interpretations that replace direct spiritual experience</note>, who would want to divide them concerning God, since they all live and exist in the one God! 

Humans in their life are all of the same quality, all begotten from one flesh and one soul, and all have but one single life, like a tree with many limbs and branches; and although the branches do not look exactly alike in form, they still have the same sap and power; so too do humans among all kinds of nations. 

Therefore, the hostile, selfish fighters for their **Babylonian opinions** <note>original: "babylonischen Meinungen" — a reference to the "confusion of tongues" at the Tower of Babel; the author views sectarian theological debates as a similar form of divine confusion and pride</note> are more foolish than the birds in the air, who all praise and honor God in one tongue and with one understanding, for they are all without images, and whatever the great God does with them, they are content with it. 

Therefore, there is no better counsel than that we become at one with ourselves again, and break and kill the **images of the letters** <note>original: "Bilder der Buchstaben" — referring to a literalist, dead understanding of scripture (the "Letter") as opposed to the living Spirit</note> and opinions within us, and desire to know nothing more of God than what He wills to know in and through us. And then the one, life-giving **Word of God**, which gives power and life to all things, is revealed to us, and all strife comes to an end, for God is our **All in All** <note>original: "Alles in Allem" — a reference to 1 Corinthians 15:28, often used by mystical writers to describe a state where the divine presence is fully realized in all of creation</note>.

### 2. Abraham and his Sons.

Sarah had to bear only one son to Abraham to signify that the kingdom of humanity is given to only **One**, and that they [humanity] belong among this One, and that in Him they should become that same One—like branches on a tree, which Christ should be in all of them. But with Keturah, Abraham fathered six sons, according to the six days of **Creation**; and Isaac—that is, Christ—is the seventh, as the **Sabbath**, in which the other six sons should enter into rest.

This story strikes down the **vain talk** <note>original: "Tand" — refers to superficial nonsense or worthless arguments</note> of those who say: "Whoever is not born into faith by nature cannot reach **Divine Sonship**" <term>Divine Sonship: original "Kindschaft Gottes," the spiritual state of being a child of God through grace rather than biological descent</term>. For it first represents Isaac as Christ and shows how the Kingdom of God belongs to Him alone as His own by inheritance, and how no human can possess it anymore by **natural right** <term>natural right: original "Naturrecht," the idea that one has a claim to salvation simply by birth or human merit</term>, because we were all cast out from it together with Adam and have lost it—just as Keturah’s children were all cast out from Abraham’s estates, and Isaac alone inherited them.

At the same time, this story represents how gifts are given to Adam’s natural children from the riches of God the Father and Christ, just as Abraham [gave] his natural children goods from

<summary>This page provides an allegorical interpretation of Abraham’s sons, casting Isaac as a symbol of Christ (the Sabbath) and Keturah’s sons as the natural world, arguing that salvation comes through spiritual inheritance rather than natural birthright.</summary>
<keywords>Abraham, Sarah, Keturah, Isaac, Christ, Sabbath, Creation, Divine Sonship, Natural Right, Adam, allegory, inheritance</keywords>

--- Page 19 ---
<meta>The previous page discussed how Abraham’s natural children (the sons of Keturah) were cast out from the primary inheritance given to Isaac, symbolizing how humanity lost its "natural right" to the Kingdom of God through Adam. It established that while Isaac alone represents the heir (Christ), gifts were still given to the others.</meta>

gave gifts of grace. For Abraham did not cast out his natural children from himself without a gift; he certainly rejected them from the **natural right** <term>natural right: original "Naturrecht," the legal or biological claim to an inheritance based on birth</term> of his estates, but not from their status as children, for according to this status they were dear to him; therefore, he gave them gifts from his possessions. In the same way, God did not cast Adam out of Paradise without his gift; first, He gave him the Messiah in the **Word of the Covenant**, and after that, He cast him out from the childlike inheritance of natural right, yet He received him back again through that gift of grace.

And just as Abraham’s natural children were not disinherited from the **Covenant** <note>In this mystical context, the "Covenant" refers to the spiritual bond and promise of salvation offered by God to all humanity</note>, but only from his worldly estates, so too is no human being disinherited from the Covenant of God established in Adam and Abraham. Every person receives the gifted Covenant while still in the mother’s womb, in that they have the power to enter into the riches of Christ within their gifted Covenant. But they do not possess these riches by natural right—to be taken by their own will—but rather as a gift. They must and shall abandon the will toward natural right and surrender that will to the Covenant; then the gift stands in the place of the self-will, and the nature of Adam lives within the gift, and nevertheless enjoys the inheritance—not in their own self-will, but in the will of the Covenant.

For the will of the Covenant inherits the **Sonship** <term>Sonship: original "Kindschaft," the spiritual state of being a child of God</term> within the natural right, but the will of the natural selfhood is cast out from it, and must—

<summary>This page explains that while humanity lost its biological claim to God's kingdom through the Fall, God provided a "gift" (the Messiah/Covenant) that allows everyone to be readopted. It emphasizes that salvation comes through surrendering the ego to this divine gift rather than through human merit or "natural right."</summary>

die to **self-will** <term>self-will: original "Eigenheit," referring to the human ego or the tendency to act independently of God's will</term>; and when that happens, he rises through the Covenant in Christ, and possesses the gift through the **right of grace** <term>right of grace: original "Gnadenrechte," a spiritual inheritance granted by God's mercy rather than by biological birthright</term>. Christ has revealed himself through the Covenant with Adam in human nature, and has become the life and will of the Covenant. Now, however, the gifted Covenant lies within all people; for just as sin was inherited from one person to all, so too the Covenant and the gift of grace are inherited from one person to all. Every person has Christ within them, but their own will does not take hold of him; instead, it crucifies him and refuses to die to **selfhood** <note>original: "Selbstheit," the state of being focused on one's own ego</note>, so that it might enter into Christ’s death and rise in the Covenant through the will of Christ.

The self-will only wants to be an adopted child of grace, and yet it is rejected before the face of God—just as Abraham cast out his natural children from his estates and disinherited them, giving the estates to Isaac alone. In this same way, the Kingdom of God belongs only to the will of the Covenant. For no one can receive the Kingdom of God unless they become a child of the Covenant, meaning they abandon the natural will and put on the will of Christ within the Covenant; only then are they a branch on the **grapevine of Christ** <note>A biblical reference to John 15:5, "I am the vine; you are the branches," emphasizing the necessity of spiritual connection to Christ</note>, receiving his spirit, will, and life.

### 5. Hagar and her son Ishmael.

Sarah with her son Isaac, and Hagar with her son Ishmael, show us how Christ and the **natural man** <term>natural man: the unregenerate human state, driven by biological impulse and ego rather than divine spirit</term> dwell together, and how the natural man, along with Ishmael and his mother, must be entirely cast out from **hereditary righteousness** <term>hereditary righteousness: original "Erbgerechtigkeit," the false belief that one is justified by birthright, merit, or natural standing</term> and from his own **self-will** <note>original: "eigenen Willen"</note>. And when the will that is surrendered to God has cast him out, then the poor nature of man sits with its rejected will in trembling fear, and resigns itself to die—just as Hagar did here with her son Ishmael after she had been cast out by Abraham. She wandered lost near **Beersheba** <note>original: "Bersaba"</note> in the desert, saw herself completely abandoned, and despaired of her and her son’s life; she sat down a stone’s throw away from the boy so that she might not have to see him die. But when she had completely surrendered herself to death, the angel came to her, called to her and comforted her, showed her a well, and told her that she should not despair unto death, for her son was yet to become a great nation.

This is to be understood as follows: when Christ is born within a person, the spiritual, newborn will rejects its own evil nature; it despises it and condemns it to death. It casts out the vanity of nature and makes the natural will into a servant, whereas it had previously been the master. But the corrupted nature stands in the

### 4. Lot’s Deliverance.

Whoever wishes to rightly understand the stories of the **First Book of Moses** <note>the Book of Genesis</note> must envision the **old and new man** <term>old and new man: a Pauline concept describing the transition from the sinful, ego-driven nature of Adam to the redeemed, spiritual nature of Christ</term> within his **mind** <note>original: "Gemüthe," the inner seat of will and emotion</note> and set Christ and Adam against one another; then he may understand everything. Otherwise, however, he understands nothing of it but a childish history. And yet it is so rich in mysteries that no human being, from the cradle to the oldest age, could fully express them, even if he possessed the intellect to do so. Such a story is also that of Lot and his deliverance.

Moses says: the men of the city pressed violently against the man Lot, and as they rushed forward and wanted to break down the door, the men <note>referring to the angels appearing as men</note> reached out and pulled Lot inside to them into the house, and shut the door. And the men at the door of the house were struck with blindness, both small and great, until they grew weary and could not find the door.

### 5. Abraham’s Willingness to Sacrifice His Son.

Thus spoke the voice of God to Abraham: Abraham! And he answered: Here I am! And God said: Take Isaac, your only son, whom you love, and go into the land of Moriah <note>original: "Maria," likely a variant spelling of Moriah</note>, and offer him there as a burnt offering upon a mountain which I will tell you. Thus the Spirit said in Moses: Your son—that is, your own will, which you love—you must sacrifice to God; you must not live for your selfhood <term>Selfhood (Selbstheit): a central concept in German mysticism referring to the ego or the selfish nature that separates a person from God</term>, but for God. For in this way, Christ was to sacrifice our human will of self-interest <term>Self-interest (Eigenheit): the state of being focused on one's own desires rather than the divine will</term> to his Father and hand it over to Him; in His death, He was to die to human selfhood for all and in all, just as when the trunk of a tree dies, all its branches also die within it. God said: "Upon a mountain which I will tell you"; that is, it was not to happen according to Abraham’s will, but rather God intended to determine it. Likewise, it should not happen with us that we would want to prescribe according to our own judgment where and how we want to sacrifice ourselves to the Father in Christ, as Babel <note>In this tradition, "Babel" refers to the "confusion" of institutionalized religion or worldly systems that operate by human reason instead of divine spirit</note> does; no, but rather upon

### 6. Joseph and his Brothers.

When Joseph had his two dreams, envy immediately arose among his brothers. They thought he would become their master; and because they were the eldest, they desired to rule over him. There one sees how the outer man <term>The outer man (der äußere Mensch): the part of the human being focused on the physical world and social status, as opposed to the "inner man" focused on the spirit.</term> has always only been concerned with the kingdom of this world. This is precisely the damage of Adam <note>This refers to the "Fall of Man," interpreted here as a psychological shift where humanity turned its attention from the internal/spiritual to the external/material.</note>—that he left the internal and strove after the external. Joseph with his coat of many colors was a youth, and moreover delicate and young, and had not yet [known] the world...

...to work in the soul of a new Joseph, so that she <note>In German, "soul" (Seele) is a feminine noun, often personified as "she" in mystical writing.</note> understands the divine mystery, looks into eternal life, and recognizes the hidden world which is yet to be revealed in man. This has also happened to this pen <note>The author refers to themselves as the "pen," an instrument being moved by a higher power.</note>, from which it receives its spirit of knowledge <term>Spirit of knowledge (Geist der Wissenschaft): A term for insight granted by divine revelation rather than through formal academic study.</term>. Now, when this person begins to speak of the hidden world of divine mysteries and to utter the wonders of God, and his brothers—being the children of the outer world in whom the hidden spiritual world has not yet been revealed—hear this, they consider it to be nonsense and fanaticism <term>Fanaticism (Schwärmerei): A derogatory term used in the 17th and 18th centuries to dismiss mystics as irrational or "swarming" with delusions.</term>. When this occurs, Reason <term>Reason (Vernunft): In this context, human intellect that relies only on sensory evidence and logic, which is blind to spiritual truths.</term> finds itself lost and does not know the ways of God, nor that it must be this way for God’s children. Reason thinks: "You seek God, and yet He leads you into distress." Then the mind <term>Mind (Gemüth): The deep seat of a person's emotions, will, and spiritual disposition.</term> begins to grieve and does not know what is happening to it; for in flesh and blood <note>original: "Fleisch und Blut"; a biblical expression for human nature in its fallen or purely physical state.</note> it does not initially understand the divine process: how Reason must become a fool, and how Christ must quite willingly take upon Himself the mockery of the Devil and the world within the person themselves. And when things proceed in this way, Reason enters into great—

Sadness and abandonment wander truly lost in the wilderness with Joseph; she <note>In the original German, "soul" (Seele) is a feminine noun, and the author continues to personify the soul's experience through feminine pronouns.</note> is fearful on every side, yet she must still carry out the Father’s command and will. This means: He also sends him a zealous Christian who recognizes his paths and comforts him, encouraging him to persevere steadfastly in God’s ways. Through this, this "new Joseph" receives courage and strength once more, finds the right path, and goes thirstily to his brothers to see what they are doing and what their intentions are. Now he places God’s commandment and will before their eyes, just as Joseph presented his father’s command. He has neither come from a university <note>original: "hohen Schule," literally "high school," but referring to an academy or university training.</note> nor has he been called to teach us. He only wants to make a name for himself, but we will throw his honor into prison and bring hatred and persecution upon him as an example <term>Example (Exempel): A public warning or deterrent used to discourage others from following the same path.</term> to others."

### 7. Adam and Christ.

As **Adam** took on a cold, false love, and shone before God with it, as if he were nonetheless God's child by his own power and will—whereby he only mocked God—so the second Adam, **Christ**, had to take all of that upon himself. He had to let himself be clothed with a **purple robe**, as a king of this world, and be mocked; for Adam had put on the purple robe of the external world and of his own power in the brilliance of **selfhood** <term>Selfhood (Selbstheit): A key term in German mysticism referring to the ego-driven nature or the state of being centered on oneself rather than on God.</term>. Now it was put on display here before **God’s wrath**; and the **white robe**, which **Herod** put on Christ in mockery, signifies the cold love, as a garment of falsehood, in which the

### 8. Christ in the Temple.

As Christ grew up in His childhood, possessing both human and divine qualities, until He was twelve years old, He went with Mary, His mother, to the feast in Jerusalem. There He entered the temple among the **scribes** <note>original: "Schriftgelehrten." These were the scholars and experts in Jewish Law.</note>, questioned them, listened to them, and gave answers to the questions of the learned men. But when His parents began their journey home, thinking He was among their traveling companions, He intentionally stayed behind with the scholars. He did not follow the intention of His parents, but rather the divine will, until they returned and sought Him. And when His mother said to Him: "My son, why have you done this to us? Behold, your father and I have sought you with great sorrow," then—

In delusion there is no perfection; the spirit must be capable of receiving the mystery, so that God’s Spirit may be the guide in its vision. Otherwise, the spirit stands only in the external mystery—such as in the external heaven of the stars—but does not possess the divine <term>magical school</term> <note>original: "magische Schule." In this mystical tradition, "magic" refers to the creative power of the spiritual will and the hidden workings of God's grace, rather than worldly sorcery.</note>, which exists only in a simple, childlike mind. The external guide \*) works and shines only in the mirror, but the internal guide \*\*) shines in the <term>essence</term> <note>original: "Wesen."</note>, which it cannot do unless God’s Spirit leads it.

---

\*) The external guide is **theoretical reason**, which occupies itself only with the knowledge of the mirror—that is, the nature of the <term>sensory world</term> <note>original: "Sinnenwelt."</note>.

\*\*) The internal guide is **practical reason**, in which reason recognizes itself as a will that is holy in itself, and without the influence of sensuality, and in which the holy will of God is revealed to us.

### 11. Knowledge of God.

All personal seeking and searching within **selfhood** <term>Selbstheit: the state of being focused on one's own ego or independent self, which the author sees as a barrier to divine union.</term> is a futile thing. One’s own will grasps nothing of God, for it is not within, but rather outside of God in its selfhood; but the will surrendered to God grasps Him, for it is not the will itself that acts, but the Spirit in which it stands still, and whose instrument it is, which reveals itself in a divine **resonance** <note>original: "Halle." This refers to a sounding echo or reverberation, often used to describe the divine Word echoing within the quieted soul.</note> within it, as much as He wills.

Everything depends on the will; the **external will** \*) must go into the internal and deny itself, just as if it were dead to the external world, though it still lives. For just as God lives and exists in the external, and the external is dead to Him so that it cannot grasp Him, so it is with you, O man! You are within the interior with your soul, but the soul's will has turned outward with **Adam** <note>A reference to the "Fall of Man," where humanity's attention shifted from spiritual unity with God to the material diversity of the world.</note>. Therefore, if you wish to behold God and eternity, turn your will back toward the interior, and then you are as God Himself: for according to the inner will, you live in God, as you were created in the beginning, and according to the outer will, you live in this world, and

---

\*) The external will is the **sensory inclination**, but the inner will is the **rational will** <note>original: "vernünftige Wille." In this context, "rational" refers to a higher, spiritual intellect or "right reason" that aligns with divine order rather than mere worldly logic.</note>, which simultaneously encompasses the will of God within itself.

<summary>This page explains that the knowledge of God is found not through self-centered seeking, but through surrendering the "external" sensory will to the "internal" spiritual will. By turning the will inward, a person returns to their original created state in union with the divine.</summary>

Then the senses bring the **power of God** to the will, and the will accepts it with joy, but also with trembling; for it recognizes itself as unworthy, because it originates from a wavering disposition, and therefore it accepts these powers only by **sinking down** <note>original: "Niedersinken." It would be better for him if he knew that God was far from him, for then he would feel no shame or mockery within himself. But since he knows that God is near him and yet he cannot grasp Him, he becomes his own enemy and brings upon himself an eternal anguish and **despair**; he remains in a dying torment and cannot **break through** <note>original: "durchbrechen." A specific mystical term describing the difficult process of the soul "breaking through" its own ego and the dark "wrath-fire" into the light of God.</note>, for nothing comes to his aid by which he might hold on and take root in the Kingdom of God.

The **Kingdom of God** stands in power, not in earthly essence; for that which is earthly is not of eternity, and therefore it is also not found in eternity. If you wish to contemplate the **heavenly essence**, see to it that you bring a **heavenly disposition** <term>Gemüth: the inner seat of the soul, encompassing the mind, emotions, and temperament.</term> to the task; then the Spirit of God will surely show you the heavenly essence. To one who is **enlightened**, it is much easier to recognize than the earthly realm. But the **Antichrist** <note>In this context, the "Antichrist" refers to a spirit of false religious pretense or intellectual pride that lacks true spiritual experience.</note> does not reach it; his shouting and calling is only **shadow-boxing** <note>original: "Spiegelfechten." Literally "mirror-fencing," meaning a sham-fight or an empty, deceptive pretense.</note>. If you do not have the right hammer, you cannot strike the bell that awakens the poor imprisoned soul. Your loud shouting does not do it; you cannot cry the divine sound into another if you do not possess it yourself.

If you raise your mind and your **disposition** and look at yourself along with all **creatures**, and think about how the **light of your life** arose within you—so that you can see the sun's brilliance, and then also, even without sunshine, see through your **imagination** <note>original: "Imagination." Here referring to the soul's power to visualize and connect with spiritual realities beyond physical sight.</note> into a vast distance where your bodily eyes cannot reach—and then think about what the cause of this might be, that you are more rational than other creatures in that you can explore all things and what is within them; and if you further ponder where the **elements**, **fire** and **air**, originated, then you will come to realize what God is, provided you are **born of God**.

For you see, feel, and perceive that all these things must have a still higher root—

Especially when you look at the **starry heavens**, you must surely ponder where they came from, how they stand so and do not shatter, nor sink above or below themselves, although there is no "above" or "below." If you then ponder what holds all this, and from what it has become, you find the **eternal birth** <note>original: "ewige Geburt." A central concept in Böhme’s thought, referring to the ongoing, timeless process by which God generates Himself and the universe.</note>, which has no beginning, and from which everything has come to be.

You also find in all things a glorious **power**, which is the life, growth, and rising of every thing; you perceive in it its beauty and gentle well-being, from which it moves. Now look at a herb or wood, and consider what its life is, by which it grows. You find therein **astringency**, **bitterness**, **fire**, and **water** <note>These are the foundational "qualities" or "source-spirits" in Böhme's alchemy of the soul. "Herbe" (astringency) is the contracting force, while "Bitter" is the expansive or stinging force.</note>. If you now separate these four things and put them back together, you will still see and perceive no growth; rather, it remains dead if it is separated from its **original mother**. Thus you see that there is an **eternal root** there, which gives all of this.

That you do not entirely see and know **God in nature** is the **cause of sin**, by which you lie enclosed within this **great divine body**; and the **power of the Godhead** is hidden from you, just as the marrow in the bones is hidden from the flesh. But if you break through in the spirit through the death of the flesh, then you see...

<vocab>**starry heavens**, **eternal birth**, **power**, **astringency** <note>original: "Herbe." Also translated as the "sour" or "harsh" quality.</note>, **bitterness**, **fire**, **water**, **original mother**, **eternal root**, **God in nature**, **cause of sin**, **power of the Godhead**</vocab>

<summary>The author explains that the stability of the stars and the growth of plants reveal an "eternal birth" and a divine root hidden within nature. He suggests that human sin acts as a barrier, hiding this divine power from our physical senses like marrow hidden inside a bone.</summary>

...you [see] the hidden God. Just as the marrow within the bones breaks through and gives the flesh power and strength, and yet the flesh cannot grasp the marrow, but only its power, so also you cannot see the hidden Godhead in the flesh; rather, you receive His power and understand through it that God dwells in you.

### 12. Faith.

Faith is not that I see; rather, faith is that I trust the hidden Spirit, and that I would sooner lose my life than not believe His promises. O, how dead is the faith of today! It stops at mere knowledge <note>original: "Wissenschaft." In this context, it refers to intellectual or academic learning rather than modern empirical science.</note>, and people think that if they know how to speak much of God, and of Christ’s merit, suffering, and death for the human race, and comfort themselves with this, that this is the way to eternal life. Oh no, it helps nothing at all that you know it and tickle yourself with it. True faith in Christ is quite another thing; it lies not merely in history and the letter <note>original: "Buchstaben." This refers to a literal or purely intellectual reading of scripture without spiritual experience.</note>, for these are only a guide and an instruction of the Word, which is living and has spirit.

True faith is the right will, which enters into the living Word. Even if you comfort yourself with Christ’s suffering, yet your will remains a rogue <note>original: "Schalk." A term for someone who is deceitful or malicious; here, it refers to a person whose inner intentions remain un-transformed by grace.</note>, then your spirit, which [comes] out of...

<vocab>God, Godhead, Faith, Spirit, Christ's Merit, eternal life, Will, Word</vocab>

For nothing pleases God except what He Himself does through our will. For there is only one single God in the essence <term>Wesen: refers to the fundamental divine substance or being</term>, and everything that works and acts with Him within that same essence is one spirit with Him. But whatever works in its own selfhood and in its own will is outside of His governance <term>Regiment: the divine order, rule, or administration</term> within itself; indeed, it is within His almighty governance, by which He rules all life, but not in the holy, divine governance; rather, it is in the governance of nature, through which He rules both evil and good. No thing is called divine that does not move and work within God's will.

There is no other will in God than to save <note>original: "selig zu machen." This carries the sense of making someone "blessed" or "eternally happy" through salvation.</note> that which is lost. Therefore, no one should despair; for if our spirit rightly raises itself, it is stronger than the abyss of hell; it can move mountains without a storm, only with its will. It is free and bound to no articles of faith, but only to true love. Within this love, faith draws its life's power and strength, and it places no importance on human opinions. It wills what God wills, for it places no value on earthly life, so that it may live in God, and so that God's Spirit may be the willing and the doing within him.

### 14. Heaven and Hell.

Humanity carries Heaven and Hell within itself in this world. A person can make an angel of themselves in God’s love and light, or an angry, false, and high-flying <note>original: "hochfliegenden." This refers to the sin of pride or Luciferian arrogance—striving to "fly" above one's station.</note> devil. Whatever the will grasps, that is what it possesses; and when the external being <note>The physical body and worldly life.</note> breaks apart in death, the will still holds that same thing within it. 

Every person has their own hell; there is nothing else that seizes them but their own spirit. It is a perpetual lifting of oneself above God in one's mind <term>mind: Original "Gemüthe," the deep seat of a person's emotions, will, and disposition.</term>, and yet it is only an eternal sinking; it seeks a ground <note>original: "Grund." A vital concept in German mysticism meaning the foundation or essence of being. Hell is "groundless" because it has abandoned God, the only true foundation.</note>, and there is none; that is its life. It is a dying and yet no dying, but only a will toward dying—an anguish within that very being which has been drawn into the will. It is an anxiety and regret over the wickedness brought in, as the soul thinks: "if only you had [done] this..."

<summary>This page describes the internal nature of Paradise and the psychological/spiritual reality of Hell as a state of the will. It emphasizes that the soul's orientation determines whether it becomes an angel or a devil after the body dies.</summary>

### 15. The Return of the Prodigal Son.

Therefore, dear children, when you pray, do not think that God dwells far from you, or that He does not hear you or see you; this is a false delusion. Reflect that it is God’s good will...

### 16. The Angels.

God created the holy angels all at once, not from foreign matter, but from Himself—out of His own power and eternal wisdom. Therefore, there is no difference between the Spirit of God and the angels, except this: that the angels are creatures <note>original: "Creaturen." In this philosophical context, this means "created beings" who have a beginning, distinguishing them from the uncreated and eternal God.</note>, and their bodily essence has a beginning; but their **power**, out of which God created them, is God Himself; it is and remains from eternity to eternity, and therefore every angel is like a little God. The angels are the children of the great God, whom He has brought forth within His body of Nature to increase divine joy. Their body is formed <note>The original German "gebil-" is cut off at the end of the page, likely intended to be "gebildet" (formed or shaped).</note> together from divine power—

I will compare them to little children who, in May when the beautiful roses bloom, go together into the lovely flowers and pluck them; they make little wreaths from them, carry them in their hands and rejoice, speaking constantly of the many varied shapes of the beautiful flowers, taking one another by the hand, and when they <unclear>come</unclear> home, they show them to their parents.

### 18. The Source of Knowledge of the Divine within Man.

Why do we quarrel so long about knowledge? For knowledge alone is not the path to blessedness <note>original: "Seligkeit." This refers to a state of spiritual bliss, salvation, or eternal happiness.</note>. The devil knows more than we do; what does that help him? Nothing! For the fact that I know much gives me no joy; but that I love my Savior Jesus Christ and always desire Him, that gives me joy, for this desire is a receiving <note>original: "Nahmen." Likely a play on the word "nehmen" (to take), suggesting that desiring God is the act of receiving or internalizing Him.</note>. In my knowledge, I do not first gather letters from many books, but rather I have the letter within me. Since heaven and earth with all their essence—and even God Himself—reside within man, should he not then be allowed to read in the book that he himself is?

Even if I had no other book than my own book, which I myself am, I would have books enough; for the entire Bible lies within me. If I have the Spirit of Christ, why do I need more books? Should I quarrel against that which is outside...

See to it only that you are born anew in the **life and spirit of Christ**; then you have everything that God is and can do.

But you are drunk, and you go astray; you seek the **key** to the book and you quarrel over the **key**. Everyone says: "I have the key," yet no one wants to unlock their own **book of life**. Everyone would have the key to God within themselves if they only sought it in the right place.

### 20. The Purpose of Christ’s Incarnation.

Outer reason <term>outer reason: human logic or intellectual understanding that is limited to the physical world and sensory experience</term> shows man only his hard, wretched, fleshy, and naked form; but it covers up the form of innocence in which man was created, in order to deceive him. Because Christ, the Son of God, has reborn us into Paradise, we should not be so negligent as to rely on human skill <note>original: "Kunst." In this period, this often referred to secular learning, artificial cleverness, or worldly science.</note> and earthly reason. We do not find Paradise and Christ within our reason; everything therein is dead and blind. We must step out of reason and enter into the incarnation of Christ; only then do we have the power from God to speak of Paradise and the Kingdom of Heaven.

But we cannot give any ground to the reason that says man was created as an earthly being. For everything that is created from earthly life alone is animal-like, and has a beginning—

...and an end, and does not reach eternity, for it does not come from eternity. Whatever is not of the eternal is transitory; it passes away like a wind that has risen and then settles again. 

It was not for the sake of such a creature <note>original: "Creatur." In this context, it refers to the temporary, physical aspect of a created being as opposed to the eternal soul.</note> that God’s Word became human. The Eternal did not enter into transitory essence for the sake of transience <term>transience: the quality of being temporary, decaying, or perishable</term> itself. Nor did it enter into the earthly realm to raise and introduce the earthly and transitory into [divine] power and majesty. 

Instead, it became human to bring back to life that which originated from power and majesty—but which had become evil and earthly, and had faded away as if in death—and to elevate it into the power of majesty, into the seat where it resided before it became a creature. It was only after man died in Adam that he became animal-like, living for this world and not for God. 

Therefore, the Word became human so that it might unite him once more entirely with God, and that Paradise might be restored within him anew. For wherever Christ enters into a person, there death and hell must yield and break apart in the inner ground <term>inner ground: "innern Grunde," a mystical term for the deepest, most essential part of the soul where the divine and human meet</term> of the soul. He destroys the Devil’s kingdom within the soul, births the soul as a child of God and as His temple, and gives the soul His will.

The **Living Word** <term>Living Word: the "Logos" or the eternal, creative power of God expressed in Christ</term> became human for this reason: so that the literal image <note>original: "buchstäbliche Bild." This refers to a superficial, purely external, or legalistic way of being that lacks spiritual depth.</note> might die, and that the first man—who was formed from the Living Word into God’s image in Christ’s Spirit, as in the Living Word—might be born anew. And once he is thus born, the **teachers of outward images** <term>Bildlehrer: those who emphasize external rituals, icons, or mental concepts rather than the living, internal presence of God</term> are more harmful than useful to him; for they only introduce their own images into the temple of Christ and destroy the image of God.

Christ came from God into this world and took our soul into Himself, so that He might lead us out of the earthliness of this world and back into God through Himself. He birthed us anew in Him, so that we might again be capable of living in God. From His will He birthed us, so that we should place our will in Him; in this way, He leads us within Himself back to the Father, into our original homeland—into Paradise, from which Adam departed. His essence, which fills the heavens, He has revealed in humanity.

But you must enter into the *doing*; otherwise, you are but a **mask** <note>original: "Larve." In this period, it meant a hollow shell, a disguise, or a creature (like a caterpillar) that has not yet reached its true form.</note> without life. Knowledge without action is like a fire that smolders but cannot burn because of the dampness. You must constantly practice your faith and always obey the voice of the Lord. And behold, though you step upon this path, you will nonetheless [experience] weakness enough...

Christ's testaments <note>In this context, "testaments" refers to the sacraments and the new covenant established by Christ.</note> are absolutely nothing other than a brotherly covenant, through which God binds Himself with us in Christ, and we in Him. ### 22. Prophecies. *)

Everyone says it will become good if only this evil would pass away. But I say to you in true knowledge, that it will not become good, but rather—

*) Quirinus Kuhlmann, in his writing *The Newly Inspired Böhme* <note>original: "Neubegeisterter Böhme"</note>, extracted and compiled one hundred and fifty prophecies from Böhme's writings. This Quirinus Kuhlmann was born in Breslau in 1651 and was already awakened by God in his 13th year, but fully enlightened in his 18th year. <meta>This page completes the prophecy regarding social discord (Section 22) and continues the biographical account of Quirinus Kuhlmann, which began in a footnote on the previous page. ---

**Böhme to Dr. Müller in Rostock.** Regarding Böhme <note>original: "Böhmen," a common variation of the name Jacob Böhme</note> and his writings, he <note>Referring to Quirinus Kuhlmann</note> had known nothing until this time. He was highly delighted to find in Böhme a man who had experienced the same insights and revelations that had been granted to him. He wrote several works but was expelled from the University of Leiden because of his <term>religious enthusiasm</term> <note>original: "Schwärmerei." In the 17th century, this term was used pejoratively by church authorities to describe those who claimed direct, personal revelation from God, which was seen as a threat to established religious order.</note>. Thereafter he went to England, Paris, and Constantinople, and published writings everywhere. Finally, he came to Prussia, Livonia, and Moscow. Here in Moscow, he became acquainted with a German merchant, Conrad Nordermann, with whom he also lived. He also wrote here and conversed with the followers of Böhme in Moscow. However, his discovery of a Jesuit conspiracy against the Tsar brought upon him their terrible vengeance, as they—with the cooperation of the Reformed and Lutheran preachers—accused him before the Patriarch as a heretic. He was thrown into prison, as was his friend, Nordermann. Blasphemous statements were extracted from his writings, and the Protestant clergy were consulted, who did not judge him favorably. Kuhlmann was then inhumanely tortured in prison. They branded several crosses onto his back with red-hot irons and reopened the wounds with vinegar and salt whenever they began to heal. After they had tortured him in this manner for three weeks, on October 4, 1689, he was—

<meta>This page continues the prophetic discourse following the account of the martyrdom of Kuhlmann and Nordermann, shifting toward a philosophical and apocalyptic explanation of the origins of worldly power and the coming unified rule.</meta>

All power and dominion <term>Herrschaft</term> of this world was not ordered from the beginning by the Supreme Good, but has grown out of the <term>Turba</term> <note>original: "Turba." In the mystical philosophy of Jacob Böhme, the *Turba* refers to the spirit of disturbance, confusion, or wrath that arises within nature when elements are out of harmony with the divine order.</note>, since nature afterward recognized it as her own essence, which was born from her, and gave it the law to continue reproducing itself within an established form of governance <term>Regiment</term>. 

Then this birth rose up until it reached the royal prerogative <note>original: "königlichen Regal," referring to the exclusive rights and powers held by a sovereign.</note>, and from then on it sought the abyss as a single entity, until it became Monarchy and Empire. But it is still rising, and desires to be One, and not Many. 

And although it exists in the Many, the first source from which all was born still desires to rule over everything, and to be the sole Lord over every form of governance. And because this craving was a form of governance in the beginning, but has divided itself over time into many parts according to the essences <note>original: "Essentien," referring to the fundamental spiritual qualities or building blocks of reality.</note>, this multiplicity now seeks the One again, and will certainly be born in the sixth number of the Crowns, as in the six-thousandth year of the symbolic figure <note>The "sixth number" refers to a prophetic timeline, common in 17th-century millenarianism, suggesting that the world’s history follows the six days of creation, with the sixth millennium being the final age of struggle before a divine Sabbath.</note>. 

This shall happen not at the very end, but in that hour of the day when the creation of wonders was completed. That is: when the wonders of the Turba reach their end, a Lord will be born who rules the entire world, but through many offices. And there, the self-grown authority <term>Obrigkeit</term> and the taskmaster <term>Treiber</term> will be sought out. For the smaller things, which were suppressed, have also run toward the goal. Now each thing separates itself, for it has reached the goal, and there is no stopping or revoking it. Also thus will

This occurs in the day and the hour when creation was placed into the wonders, within the Mystery and into the Mystery, as a mirror of eternity. ### 25. The Self-Will that Opposes God.

Every will that enters into its own **selfhood** <term>Selbstheit</term> <note>The state of being focused entirely on the "I" or ego, which Böhme identifies as the root of all sin and suffering.</note> tears itself loose from God; it wants to be its own **regime** <term>Regiment</term> <note>meaning an autonomous rule or self-governance independent of divine order.</note>, but that is contrary to God, for He alone is All. Therefore understand, O human, what you are to do; examine yourself within yourself, what you are, and whether you are united with the will of Him who created you. If not, then know that you are a renegade, disobedient child, and have made yourself into an enemy, in that you have entered into your own desire and will, and have made yourself into **private property** <term>Eigenthum</term> <note>Here, "property" refers to the illusion of self-possession—the idea that our lives and souls belong to ourselves rather than to God.</note>, and therefore do not dwell in God as in your first Mother, but—

<meta>catchword: but- (son-)</meta>

<summary>This page presents a prophecy of global spiritual renewal under a "simple shepherd" and warns that judgment is coming for those rooted in "selfhood." It defines the fall of man as the act of turning the will away from God to become an autonomous, self-owned entity.</summary>

And if He finds your will in Him, then He reveals Himself to your will as His own property. Everything that you then investigate, you see in His light, and you are His.

### 26. The True Christian.

A true Christian breaks apart **selfhood** <term>Selbstheit</term> <note>In Böhme’s theology, "Selbstheit" refers to the egoistic, self-centered nature of fallen man that seeks to be its own master instead of submitting to the Divine.</note> like a vessel in which he lies imprisoned, and blossoms forth <note>original: "grünet... aus," literally "greens out."</note> with his will, surrendered to God, into the will of God—just as a beautiful flower grows out from the earth—and works with and in God that which pleases God. He falls to the ground before his Creator and lives in **humility** <term>Demuth</term>. No other will attains **Christ’s inheritance**. **Great knowledge** <term>Vielwissen</term> does not achieve it either; the shepherd in the field is as near to God as a scholar <note>original: "Doctor." Böhme frequently contrasts simple piety with the intellectualism of university-trained theologians.</note>; no subtlety in quarreling over the way of God helps at all; rather, it is a hindrance and a delay.

Therefore, dear brothers, guard yourselves against quarreling over the **form of the letter** <term>Buchstabenform</term> <note>A reference to literalist or dogmatic disputes over the "dead letter" of scripture versus the living spirit.</note>, and against contempt. A true Christian has nothing to quarrel about, for he dies to his outward **rational desire** <term>Vernunftbegehren</term>; he desires only God’s knowledge in His love and grace, and lets go of everything that quarrels over form; for the Spirit of Christ must [the]...

As a Christian, a servant of God must not look to the example of his ancestors who walked in the lust of earthly life. Whoever does this is still far from the kingdom of God and cannot call himself a true Christian with a good conscience; for he stands only in the **form of Christendom** <term>Christenheit</term>, and not in the **Spirit of Christ**. The form shall break apart and cease with time, but the Spirit remains standing eternally.

### 27. The Antichrist.

All strife and war, and envy and hatred, have arisen from the world through the Antichrist, who desires to be honored as a god in the form of an angel, but inside him dwells the devil. You already see this Antichrist in Cain, who struck down his brother for the sake of faith <note>Böhme often interprets the biblical Cain and Abel as symbols of the "false" institutional church persecuting the "true" spiritual church.</note>. Oh, you dear peoples on earth, you are all of one flesh; but that you have divided yourselves from one another, that is the work of the devil through the Antichrist. Your fear of God has been great at times, but out of love, gratitude, and good intentions, you have bestowed too much honor upon men; therefore, they have stepped out from themselves <note>Meaning they have abandoned their humble, inner spiritual center.</note> into desires and worldly honor. 

Look indeed, dear brothers, how very deceitfully <note>original: "diebisch," literally "thievishly"; suggesting they have stolen spiritual authority that belongs only to God.</note> they have dealt with you. They have set themselves over the earth and have claimed for themselves heaven and hell and all power and honor, blinding you with **hypocrisy** <term>Gleißnerei</term>. They have incited you to strife and war, so that you have murdered one another and laid waste to your fatherland. 

Look at the **Papacy** <term>Pabstthum</term> in Rome, how it has blinded the whole world, how it has made one religious order and one opinion holier than another, and how it sold the orders of the high estates for money. The simple layman was persuaded that this was holiness, and thus he prayed before the dragon in the **Book of Revelation** <note>A reference to the Beast in Revelation 13, which Böhme and other Reformation-era thinkers often identified with the corruption of the Roman Church.</note> and sought **indulgences** <term>Ablaß</term> from him.

<summary>This page identifies the "Antichrist" not as a single person, but as the spirit of religious pride, division, and institutional corruption that leads to war. It specifically critiques the Roman Papacy for selling spiritual favors and inciting conflict among the "one flesh" of humanity.</summary>

<keywords>Antichrist, Cain, religious war, hypocrisy, Papacy, Rome, Book of Revelation, indulgences, spiritual corruption</keywords>

### 28. Germany’s Antichrist.

People think that we have now departed from the Antichrist in Germany with the **champion** <note>original: "Streiter"; likely referring to a specific reformer or religious leader who was expected to defeat the "Antichrist" of the papacy.</note>, but it is not yet so. For those who curse the Antichrist and confront him with his shame are also grown from the Antichrist's tree; they are the Antichrist's bears and wolves who suck him dry and devour him, for the spirit of the antichristian principle has commanded them to do so. The opinions regarding the **Cup** <note>original: "Kelch"; a reference to the fierce theological debates over whether the laity should receive the wine during Communion, a major point of contention in the Reformation.</note> and the **person of Christ** <note>Referring to the "Christological controversies" regarding how the divine and human natures of Jesus interacted.</note> that are currently circulating in Germany are also grown from the antichristian tree; they are the Antichrist's children, whom he guides with fine subtlety. Oh, what an artist the Devil is! If you do not open your eyes, this will continue until the end.

### 29. How the Devil or Sin Seeks to Suppress the Good; Böhme explains this from his own experience.

Because I am now dealing with the article of **repentance** <term>Buße; in Böhme’s thought, this is not just saying sorry, but a fundamental "turning around" of the soul toward God</term>, I tell the reader that in my earnestness, this pen was given to me, which the **driver** <term>Treiber; a taskmaster or oppressor. ---

*) The meaning of this passage is: Through my earnest striving for divine knowledge, God has granted it to me and enabled me to write about divine things. Finally, after seven years, God gave me insight and courage again, so that I began to write anew, with the resolution to let nothing dissuade me from it anymore.

When he no longer practiced contemplation and writing, he naturally lost his good insights and also his imaginative visions. Nevertheless, he ceased his writing for seven years <note>This refers to the "years of silence" (1612–1619) after the authorities in Görlitz, prompted by the clergy, forbade Böhme from writing following the controversy of his first book, *Aurora*.</note>, and despite his unrest, he would likely have ceased it forever had not external causes determined him to begin anew and continue until his end. To Böhme himself, however, several noblemen presented the matter in writing as a matter of conscience and encouraged him not to bury his talent <note>original: "sein Pfund nicht zu vergraben"; a reference to the biblical Parable of the Talents in Matthew 25:14-30.</note>. And this moved him to write again, regardless of what might happen to him as a result.

### 30. Christian Improvement.

The true way into eternal life is within man; he has led the soul’s will out into the external world, and he must make it flourish <note>original: "grünen"; literally "to turn green" or "to bloom," a common Böhme metaphor for spiritual growth.</note> again within himself, as in the inner man. But he has a bitter way to go, for the will is supposed to leave the external world, and yet it cannot do so entirely, for it must nourish the external body.

<meta>This page continues the discussion from page 105 regarding the freedom of the will and the soul's surrender. The previous page established that the will toward good or evil arises within the individual, and this page explains how external influences affect that internal will.</meta>

Through external occurrences of evil and good, the **creature** <note>In Böhme’s theology, a "creature" is any created being, emphasizing our status as part of God's creation rather than self-derived beings.</note> is infected, just as if a poisonous external air were to infect and poison the body. In this same way, external things corrupt the creature's own will, causing it to attach itself to either evil or good. And for this reason, God has given humanity teachings and laws, so that through these commandments, one might find the motivation to reject evil influences. He must know that he is just as capable of choosing the good as he is the evil, and that God is not the cause of humanity’s fall.

But since the will has abandoned God’s will and entered into its own personal will, there is no other counsel than this: by the Spirit which was breathed into him by God, he must step out of <term>selfhood</term> <note>German: *Selbstheit*. This refers to the state of being self-centered or trapped in one's own ego, which Böhme sees as the root of sin.</note>—that is, out of his own natural will—and return completely and entirely to the original Will. He must die completely to his <term>I-ness</term> <note>German: *Ichheit*. Similar to "selfhood," this refers to the subjective ego that seeks its own power rather than God's.</note> and cast himself entirely into God’s will and mercy, so that he no longer lives or wills for himself, but for God, who created him in the original Will according to His own image. Because the "I-ness" resists this, and the selfhood refuses to die, one must feed the inner will of the spirit with **God’s food** <note>This refers to spiritual nourishment, such as the Word of God or the sacraments, which strengthens the soul against worldly desires.</note> so that it may live without hungering for external things. Thus, it constantly puts to death the will of the earthly "I-ness" until the physical body freely yields itself in death and returns once more to the mother from whom it was created—

### 33. Good and Evil, Law and Sin.

A thing that is **One** <note>Böhme uses "One" to describe the primordial state of God or the soul in perfect union with the Creator—a state where no internal conflict or external law is necessary.</note> has neither commandment nor laws. But if it mixes with another, then there are two beings in One, and there are also two wills, where one runs against the other, from which **enmity** <note>Hostility or conflict that arises when a created being's will diverges from the universal divine will.</note> arises. In this way, sin in human life is to be fathomed. For life is unified and good, and God dwells in the highest life of man. But everything that is to exist in God must be free from its **own will** <note>German: *eigenen Willen*. A central Böhme concept: the ego-driven desire to exist for oneself rather than as an instrument of God's spirit.</note>. Its will must be unified in God, so that God’s and man’s will and spirit are but One. For what is One does not oppose itself, for it has only One will. Wherever that will goes, or whatever it does, it is One with itself.

God dwells in all things, but nothing grasps Him unless it is One with Him. But if it departs from the One, it goes out of God into itself, and is now something other than God, and separates itself. There the **law** <note>In this context, the "law" is the spiritual pressure or requirement for the soul to abandon its separate ego and return to the divine unity.</note> arises: that it should once again go out from itself into the One, or else it shall be separated from the One. In this way, it is to be recog-

Now there are two lords, and they separate one from the other. Therefore, everything that desire possesses for its own is sin and **opposition** <note>German: *Widerwille*. ...grow through the **resistance** <note>German: *Widerwille*. In Böhme’s philosophy, this "contrariety" or "opposition" is the friction between the self-will and the divine will which, when overcome, leads to spiritual growth.</note> which surrenders itself back to the One, and which breaks and kills that resistance. And as man is born anew through Christ, so his will—which had departed into error and **multiplicity** <note>German: *Vielheit*. This refers to the fragmented nature of the material world as opposed to the singular unity of God.</note>—dwells once again in God.

Yet this multiplicity must break along with the body and die away from the will that emerged from it; then this outgoing will is recognized as a **new birth** <note>German: *neue Geburt*. A central concept in Böhme’s theology, referring to the spiritual transformation of the soul.</note>. For it takes everything back into the One, yet not with its own **desire** <note>German: *Begierde*. For Böhme, this is a "drawing in" or selfish craving that creates matter and sin.</note>, but with its own love, which is unified in God, so that God may be "All in All" and His single will may be the will of all things. For this is the law and eternal essence of eternity: that it has only one will. If it had two, one would break the other, and there would be conflict. But its life is love alone, from which light and majesty proceed.

### 34. Forgiveness of Sins and Divine Service.

When we find and recognize ourselves, we also see and recognize what God is and what He is capable of. We see that He does not forgive sins out of mere favor, in the way a king grants a pardon to a criminal who has forfeited his life. No, it is not a matter of playing the hypocrite and remaining a **rogue** <note>German: *Schalk*. An archaic term for a wicked, deceptive, or worldly person.</note>. It means [emerging] from God—

God requires no service for His own benefit; instead, we are to love one another and serve one another, while thanking, honoring, praising, and calling upon the great God.

The **devil** does not let go of your soul until she leaves everything earthly behind for him. When she does this, she departs from his territory, and he is overcome. Like a **fowler** <note>a bird-catcher</note> he is always chasing her; if he can only manage it, he dresses her in that earthly garment once more. What a heavy struggle the poor soul must endure with the devil! And when he has captured the poor soul again and will not let her go, but drags her down into the **abyss of despair**, then the soul must seize hold of Christ’s suffering and death. She must walk with the devil through hell into the death of Christ, and from Christ’s death, she must **sprout anew** <note>German: *ausgrünen*. How does it help you to pray that God should forgive you for Christ’s sake, when you yourself do not forgive? Your heart remains full of revenge and injustice. You go into the church, into the congregation of Christ, and you lead a deceiver, a hypocrite, a liar, a greedy man, an angry man, an unchaste man, and a proud man inside—and then you lead him right back out again. What benefit do you gain from this? You go to the **Lord’s Supper** <note>German: *Abendmahl*. The sacrament of the Eucharist or Communion</note> and desire the body and blood of Christ, yet you still give lodging to the black devil within you. What do you truly think? Will you receive anything other than the stern **wrath of God**?

### 35. Punishment and Reward in and after Death.

Now when the final hour of death arrives and the **conscience** awakens, the poor soul trembles for fear of the **torment of hell**, and would then also like to be saved. But there is little **faith** present; instead, there is sheer **unrighteousness**. Sins stand before the person and read to him the book of his conscience. And even if the **mind** <term>German: *Gemüth*. In mystical thought, this refers to the seat of the soul's desires, emotions, and will—the very core of the inner person.</term> wishes to turn to God, sin strikes it down again, and great doubt and unrest arise within the soul. It is meant to press through to God out of the kingdom of hell through **Christ's death**, but hell still holds it by a great bond, for falsehood has not yet been washed away. Christ indeed calls the poor soul, but its **lamp** is still unadorned. <note>A reference to the Parable of the Ten Virgins (Matthew 25:1-13); an "unadorned" lamp is one without the oil of faith and spirit, unprepared for the bridegroom’s arrival.</note> Yet by the thread of faith in Christ, the soul can still obtain confidence in God and be snatched from its ruin. But although it may reach the joys of heaven, it will not be equal in **blessedness** to those who turned to God early with their whole heart.

### 56. Spiritual Trial, Tribulation, and Doubt.

Dear child, if you wish to become God’s child, then prepare yourself for **spiritual trial** <term>German: *Anfechtung*. A central term in German theology, particularly for Jakob Böhme and Martin Luther, referring to a period of intense spiritual anxiety, doubt, or a sense of being under attack by the devil or one's own conscience.</term> and tribulation. It is not easy or gentle to enter into the divine life while **reason** <term>German: *Vernunft*. In this mystical context, "reason" refers to the limited, logical mind of man that is bound to the physical world and cannot perceive divine truths on its own.</term> remains imprisoned in the earthly realm. It must be broken, and the will must emerge from reason and sow itself into God’s kingdom in humble obedience, just as a grain is sown into the field. Then the Holy Spirit helps it to endure the struggle against earthly reason and against the will of the corrupted flesh.

Thus, because reason cannot look upon God, it must be constrained within hope. Then doubt runs up against faith and seeks to destroy hope. Then it is said: Be sober, watch, fast, and pray, so that you might dull the earthly reason and God’s Spirit may find a place within you. When the Spirit appears, He soon overcomes the earthly reason and gazes upon the will with His love and **sweetness** <note>German: *Süßigkeit*. A common term in mystical writing to describe the overwhelming experiential "taste" of God's presence and grace.</note>; then, a beautiful little branch is always born from the **tree of faith** <note>The "Tree of Faith" is a metaphor for the organic growth of the soul as it matures through struggle and divine nourishment.</note>, and all tribulation and spiritual trials serve for the best interest of God’s children.

Therefore, no one should think that the tree of Christian faith can be seen or recognized in the kingdom of this world; the outward reason

### 38. Labor, Avarice, and Trust in God.

Man must work and earn, for he was created into this outer life for this purpose: that through his <term>skill</term><note>original: *Kunst*. In the 17th century, this referred to any specialized knowledge, craft, or technical ability, not just the "fine arts."</note> and activity, he should reveal the <term>wonders of God</term><note>original: *Wunder Gottes*. A central concept in this philosophy, suggesting that the physical world and human inventions are outward manifestations of God's hidden, inner power.</note>. And whoever trusts in God, He blesses in body and soul, in the field, in the house, and in his trade. In whatever he then does, the Spirit of God is within and active. How should He abandon the body that is meant to reveal God’s wonders? He is well off in all things, provided it is not false and does not run against the love of God and man; and if someone were to carry stones into the sea, if it pleases his brother and he finds his sustenance in it, he is as dear to God as another who performs a greater work.

Man has free will; he may delight himself on earth in whatever work he chooses; everything stands within God’s wonders, let man do what he will! A shepherd is as dear to Him as a <term>doctor</term><note>original: *Doktor*. Here referring to a highly educated scholar or theologian, representing the pinnacle of social and intellectual status.</note>, if only he is pious and trusts in God. The simple person is as useful to Him as the wise person, for with the wise He rules

### 39. The Holy Supper.

All conflict regarding the Person of Christ and his bequeathed <term>testaments</term><note>original: *Testamenten*. The soul, in its innermost part, is nourished only by the Word and Essence of God; for it came into the body from God's Word and was breathed into it, and therefore it can only receive its nourishment from that same source.
